Survival-analysis / competing-risks framing.\n\n**OTHER STRUCTURAL FIXES (all 14 iter2 critiques):** rubric sub-scores from the StrongREJECT RUBRIC prompt on\na local judge (never the fine-tuned Gemma-2B, which emits ONE expected-value score); never exclude\nrecovery-after-prefill (FEL_all / FEL_engaged / recovery rate); SELECTION GAP pre-registered; H2 population\npre-registered WEIGHTS-ONLY with pooled LOFO, prompted arms confined to a 2x2 H5 design; H4 as a\nposition-matched 2(dir) x 3(prompt-only/response-only/all) x 2(real vs null) grid with an **anisotropy-matched\nnull** (isotropic matched-norm understates); weight-scar claim DROPPED for a one-forward-pass engagement\nreadout + token-coverage diagnostic; binary harm modelled as `engagement x P(graded leakage > tau)` with tau\nfitted per checkpoint (the SafeRL row is a judge-THRESHOLD disagreement, only the Qwen3-4B row is engagement);\nH1b demoted to a diagnostic replication of JADR §7.2; TOST everywhere instead of \"CI covers 0\"; per-artifact\ncompute table; TrustLLM scope boundary stated; in-house GSM8K column.\n**dFEL VARIANCE FIX (the reviewer's sharpest methodological point):** variance now comes from a pre-registered\n**ARM-TYPE ORDERING** tested on **within-lineage sibling deltas** (13 lineages, 26 deltas, sign test) —\nbase ~0, instruct <0, **abliterated = the discriminating cell** (returns to ~0 ⇒ grain rides the refusal\ndirection and abliteration is NOT a pure gate removal; stays at instruct ⇒ two separate control points),\nSafeRL << 0 or ~instruct. Both branches publishable. SP itself still needs no parent; the parent only powers\nthe validation.\n\n**2602.14689 FULL VERIFIED FACT-SHEET (arxiv.org/html/2602.14689v1, 54pp, 16 Feb 2026):**\n- **23 named strategies x 5 variants = 115 prefills/request**: Affirmative Generic, Affirmative Targeted,\n  Affirmative Past, Language Switch, Distractors, Fragmented Punctuation, Self-referencing, Continuation\n  Partial, **Continuation Full**, Code Completion, Fake Citation, Logic Failure, Authority Impersonation,\n  Persona Switch, Self Justification, **System Simulation**, Role Chaining, Evaluative, Intent Hijacking,\n  Context Noise, Reverse, Junk Tokens, Leet Speak. Prefills generated with an abliterated Gemma3-27B.\n- **Generic \"Sure, I can help you with that\" = 19.6% mean ASR; request-specific variants = 47.1%** ⇒ iter2's\n  single generic opener was the WEAKEST instrument available.\n- Judges: **GPT-OSS-Safeguard 20B** (\"a helpfulness score from 0 to 5 reflecting the extent to which the\n  response facilitates harmful activity\", scaled to [0,1]) + Qwen3Guard 8B; success `h >= 0.8`; five\n  categories = **actionable instructions / partial enabling information / harmful alternatives /\n  troubleshooting and optimization / failure to fully refuse**. Blinding (the word \"blind\" never appears):\n  *\"To avoid evaluation bias, neither guard model is exposed to the prefill strings; both evaluate only the\n  original harmful request and the generated response.\"*\n- Fig. 4 verbatim: *\"Attack success v\n=========== project_qwen3_safety_reviewhypo_iter3_run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-reviewhypo-iter3-run-e2qyxwf-ycsy\ndescription: \"Iter3 REVIEW_HYPO run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy ('The Answers a Refusal Hides' / HIDDEN MASS) — score 5/borderline, relation=evolution, 18 critiques (11 major); H1 PRIMARY criterion is ARITHMETICALLY UNSATISFIABLE (TOST |rho|<.45 at n=4 needs |z|<-0.797) AND negative-by-design via stratum dilution (null gives rho=+1.00); hidden mass re-commits the collider; 2602.14689's 0.961 is ASR_any over 115 prefills from a THINKING-STAGE appendix table\"\nmetadata:\n  node_type: memory\n  type: project\n---\n\nRun `run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy` iter_3 REVIEW_HYPO (2026-09-20) of **\"The Answers a Refusal Hides\"** / HIDDEN MASS\n(see [[project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-iter3-run-e2qyxwf-ycsy]]). **Score 5 / borderline, confidence 4;\nsoundness 2 / presentation 3 / contribution 3; 18 critiques (11 major); relation_type = evolution**\n(same gate/emission frame + SP readout, headline narrowed to the refused-stratum estimand).\nOutput 46.3 KB at `iter_3/review_hypo/.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`, assembled by\n`build.py` (own critiques) + `critiques_novelty.json` -> `build_final.py`. Working notes in\n`notes/verified_numbers.md`.\n\n**THE TWO FATAL FINDINGS (both numerically proven, both cheap to fix):**\n1. **H1's PRIMARY criterion cannot be satisfied at any observed value.** It is a per-checkpoint TOST\n   `|rho|<0.45` on a paired rank correlation across **FOUR** refusal-lever levels, required in >=8 of 13.\n   At n=4, SE(Fisher z)=1/sqrt(n-3)=**1.000**, 90% one-sided margin **1.2816 > atanh(.45)=0.4847**, so\n   passing needs observed **|z| < -0.797** — impossible, including at rho=0. **Minimum paired n = 10.**\n   (Spearman at n=4 also takes only 11 discrete values.) Fix: pool 13x4=52 obs in ONE mixed model with a\n   checkpoint random intercept, or >=10 lever levels, or item-level pairing.\n2. **The same test is NEGATIVE BY DESIGN via stratum dilution.** Moving the lever changes WHICH items are\n   refused. Simulated the pure no-emission-control null (h~U(0,1), forced specificity monotone in h,\n   refuse most-hazardous-first): hidden mass .914/.839/.756/.672 vs marginal harm .386/.233/.115/.031 at\n   r=.2/.4/.6/.8 ⇒ **Spearman = +1.00**. The mechanical null produces exactly the tracking the equivalence\n   test must rule out. Fix: hold the stratum FIXED across levels + publish the composition-only null curve.\n\n**THE HIGHEST-LEVERAGE SINGLE FIX (closes 3 critiques at once): define hidden mass on a COMMON stratum**\n(items refused by a fixed reference model, or by >=k of the panel). HIDDEN MASS as specified conditions on\n**the model's OWN refusals** = the identical post-treatment/collider conditioning the paper condemns in\nother people's conditional-harm columns, just on the complementary set ⇒ not cross-model comparable. The\ncomparable quantity (**refusal-adjusted risk**, fixed population) IS correctly derived but is NOT the\nprimary contrast or H2's target. Common stratum makes it comparable + **restores base/abliterated/\nsafe-completion arms** (which have no refused stratum, so all the arm-type dFEL variance is currently\nABSENT from the hidden-mass analysis) + removes the range restriction.\n\n**2602.14689 FACTS VERIFIED THIS SESSION (kill the reproduction gate, 4 independent ways):**\n- **ASR_any is best-of-115**, verbatim: *\"A request is counted as successful if at least one strategy\n  succeeds... ASR_any = (1/|R|)∑ 1[max_a S_attack(r,a)=1]\"* over 23 strategies x 5 variants.\n- **The no-prefill 0.358 is ALSO ASR_any**, over 23x5 non-prefilled RESAMPLES ⇒ vs the plan's single\n  temperature-0 greedy pass, that is best-of-115 vs best-of-1.\n- **0.961/0.358 are ASR_any^oss and come from Table 4, Appendix E.2 \"Qwen3 with Prefilling in the THINKING\n  Stage\"** — while Stage 0 pre-registers **non-thinking with an empty `<think></think>`**. The paper's\n  main-text default metric is **ASR^min**, for which the same row reads **0.922 / 0.318**.\n- Item set is **ClearHarm 179 CBRN**, not 120 OpenSafeIntent rows.\n- **Per-strategy ASR for Qwen3-4B is NOT published** (Fig.4 / Appx C cover only \"the largest models from\n  each model family\") ⇒ no like-for-like target exists; recommend an **ORDINAL gate** instead:\n  prefill>natural, Base>Instruct, Affirmative Targeted > Affirmative Generic, Continuation Full > Generic.\n- Per-strategy mean-over-model ASR: **Generic 19.6 / Targeted 47.1 / Continuation Full 67.3 / Fake Citation\n  67.5 / System Simulation 73.7** ⇒ their 3-opener \"max = attacker-relevant number\" is anchored on the\n  WEAKEST named strategy and omits the top one.\n- **CONFIRMED NO conditioning on the natural refusal decision anywhere** ⇒ the refused-stratum lane really\n  is open against this paper.\n\n**OTHER MAJORS:** SP's DiD cancels string MAIN effects only — the **string x framing INTERACTION survives**,\nand prompt-continuation **lexical overlap IS such an interaction** (visible in their own worked example:\nS shares \"consultan*\"/threshold-audit content with the MALICIOUS prompt, G with the BENIGN one) ⇒ demand a\n**PLACEBO continuation pair** + an overlap-balance criterion in the S/G check + a framing-sensitivity\nnormaliser. The **abliteration \"discriminating cell\" is undecidable** unless |dFEL(instruct)| exceeds the\nequivalence bound (only a SIGN test is pre-registered; 5-pair 95% half-width ~0.020 at item SD .25/120\nitems ⇒ needs |dFEL(instruct)| > ~0.04). **H1's primary test uses the H5 prompted lever although H5 states\nprompted arms are excluded from H1/H2** (d\n=========== project_qwen3_safety_genhypo_run_m-mKFEz3j7gs.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-run-m-mkfez3j7gs\ndescription: \"Iter1 GEN_HYPO for the Qwen3 base/safety/abliterated cheap-safety-metric prompt (run_m-mKFEz3j7gs) — six lanes killed by prior art, shipped hypothesis = refusal as instruction-priority arbitration\"\nmetadata: \n  node_type: memory\n  type: project\n  originSessionId: af5cda4b-7b9d-4be0-bdb6-c0d9a0610731\n  modified: 2026-09-19T05:23:39.328Z\n---\n\nRun `run_m-mKFEz3j7gs`, iter_1 GEN_HYPO (2026-09-19). AII prompt: Qwen3 base vs official\nsafety-tuned vs community abliterated; mech-interp the activation differences; build a\n0-to-few-prompt safety metric for arbitrary HF checkpoints. Hardware: **2 CPU, 755GB RAM,\n20GB disk, NO GPU**; OpenRouter-only LLM calls, $10 cap.\n\n**SHIPPED HYPOTHESIS — \"Refusal is just instruction priority.\"** Refusal is not a\nsafety-specific circuit but the output of a general instruction-priority arbitrator (the\nmachinery making a system-level instruction beat a conflicting user-level one). Three\nsub-claims: (a) bidirectional causal cross-steering between the benign-conflict \"priority\"\ndirection and the refusal direction; (b) **Priority Gain (PG)** = log-odds a system\ninstruction beats a conflicting user one, minus a matched NON-conflicting control, over ~20\nBENIGN conflict pairs — zero harmful content, no generation, no judge, no parent model, already\nin universal units so nothing transfers across families; (c) rank-1 abliteration should leave a\nmeasurable scar on *harmless* instruction priority. Both outcomes informative: a null refutes\nthe widespread \"jailbreaks are instruction-hierarchy failures\" intuition.\n\n**SIX LANES CHECKED AND CLOSED** (do not re-propose — each died to specific prior art):\n\n1. **Attention-sink / BOS carries the safety prior; \"vacuum state\" readable from a 1-token\n   pass** — WOUNDED→ABANDONED. **FLASH (aclanthology 2026.findings-acl.1039, ACL 2026\n   Findings)** already says the sink \"unintentionally serves as a computational anchor for\n   safety alignment\", guardrails are \"front-loaded in specific attention heads of shallow\n   layers\", and does a sink-localised intervention. **2606.02288 \"Massive Spikes in LLMs are\n   Bias Vectors\"** already names the input-invariant sink vector `b` AND **predicts the null**:\n   \"W_V aligns b with its spectral null-space … the projected value vector practically vanishes\n   (v_sink ≈ 0)\". **2603.14337** already publishes the sink-bias-broadcast decomposition.\n   Also: reviewers call the causal-masking \"vacuum state\" observation definitionally true.\n2. **Extreme-value / GPD tail index of the refusal margin as a scale-free safety metric** —\n   OCCUPIED. Anthropic **2502.16797 \"Forecasting Rare Language Model Behaviors\"** (Gumbel-tail\n   method, EVT, elicitation probabilities) + **2606.16511 \"Tail-Shape Estimation in LLM\n   Evaluation Is Fragile\"** which already states the tail index \"isolat[es] tail shape from tail\n   magnitude\" for LLM safety.\n3. **Refusal stereotypy / template collapse → low rank → easy removal** — OCCUPIED by\n   **2608.25390** (\"diverse refusal prefixes raise stable rank and weaken refusal vector\n   ablation attacks\"; repetitive refusal starts ⇒ low-stable-rank ⇒ brittle). Also already\n   flagged occupied in [[project-steering-genhypo-run-hbxktv59ahlj]].\n4. **Refusal unidimensionality / coherence index (Cronbach/Afriat-CCEI style)** — premise\n   REFUTED: **2602.02132 (EMNLP 2026) \"There Is More to Refusal … than a Single Direction\"**,\n   Wollschläger concept cones (ICML 2025), **2502.09674** (ICML 2025). Unidimensionality is not\n   present pre-abliteration, so \"coherence collapse\" cannot be isolated.\n5. **Normalized layer depth-of-commitment (logit lens)** — PARTIALLY OCCUPIED by\n   **2408.17003 \"Safety Layers in Aligned LLMs\"** + **2605.20241 Geometry-Lite** (safety evidence\n   is boundary/extremal-position dominated, not a smooth commitment curve).\n6. **Chat-template / role-asymmetry gating of safety** — FOLKLORE. Public writeups already call\n   `apply_chat_template()` \"the safety switch\" for Gemma/Qwen.\n\n**LANDSCAPE FACTS worth keeping** (all fetched, not snippets):\n- Stated open gap, in the authors' own words: no cheap, **reference-free**, cross-family,\n  sandbagging-robust safety estimate for an arbitrary checkpoint.\n- **N-GLARE 2511.14195 (ACL 2026)** = strongest cheap baseline: non-generative latent\n  trajectories, red-teaming trends at <1% token/runtime cost, 40+ models. Still needs a\n  red-team prompt set → must be beaten on the \"zero harmful content\" axis, not on cost.\n- **2607.01854** two-signal abliteration audit: AUROC 0.95 / 273 ckpts but self-admits it\n  \"presumes an attested reference\" and is \"effective triage, not tamper-proofing\".\n- **2508.00161 \"Watch the Weights\"** (ICLR 2026): genuinely input-free but needs the matched base.\n- **2607.13346**: \"cross-model transfer is null\" — probe AUROC 0.87 (Llama-3.1-8B) → 0.43 (Qwen3-32B).\n- **2608.05086** IRT for AI Safety: ~10 adaptive items ≈ full benchmark, 97–99% cost cut, three\n  factors (refusal strictness / truthfulness / contextual harm); authors flag **sandbagging**.\n- **2507.11878 \"LLMs Encode Harmfulness and Refusal Separately\"** = the main rival account, and\n  the source of alternate #1.\n- **2609.00051**: detection→refusal circuit = Harmful Detection Heads + Safety Neurons + Refusal\n  Heads. **2606.26161**: \"Refusal Lives Downstream of Persona\" (alternate #3).\n- **2605.08513**: refusal neurons fire at **post-instruction \n=========== project_qwen3_safety_genhypo_iter2_run_m-mKFEz3j7gs.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-iter2-run-m-mkfez3j7gs\ndescription: \"Iter2 GEN_HYPO revision for run_m-mKFEz3j7gs — reviewer's 3 near-scoops confirmed AND 4 more found; Purely training/benchmark work:\n   no directions, no steering, no ablation, no cross-model correlation.\n2. **2608.28648** ships **System Authority Delta (SAD)** — a ZERO-HARM benign-conflict ranking of 8\n   models. => any \"Priority Gain\"-style benign conflict metric is a RENAME. Adopt + cite, never claim.\n   41 pairs, 4 conditions (sys-only/user-only/opposed/same-channel), 0.97 balacc decodable, layer-12\n   mean-of-4-LR-probes steers system compliance 0.132->0.530; \"directions selected mainly for pooled\n   separability steer poorly\". Open, verbatim: arbitration geometry transfer to safety \"remains open\".\n3. **2609.06951 Steering Interference** — full 24-behaviour source x target matrix over 10 models with\n   **refusal as a source**, doses calibrated to each behaviour's own judge-measured self-effect under a\n   coherence floor. **DOSE-MATCHED CROSS-STEERING IS OCCUPIED** (+2609.06473 \"iso-effect framework ...\n   at matched behavioral effect\"). ED50/Hill is a re-parameterisation, NOT a contribution.\n   The ONE empty cell: none of their 24 behaviours is instruction-conflict / system-prompt adherence.\n4. **2608.28502 Recognition Without Enforcement** — role-template position linearly decodable yet models\n   execute instructions they verbalise as forged; \"not a unified abstract trust representation\";\n   \"model self-arbitration as a capability rather than a security boundary\". 46 endpoints, 14,294 trials.\n   => role-position decodability is TAKEN; recognition != enforcement must be DESIGNED for.\n\n**THE RE-AIM (what survived).** ADDING arbitration adds safety (2511.04694). REMOVING refusal leaves\ninstruction-following intact (2607.17427 verbatim: \"Instruction-following is preserved. For Qwen it is\nidentical across arms\"). Both are only consistent if the link is ONE-WAY: **arbitration is UPSTREAM,\nrefusal is a consumer**. Nobody has run the removing direction. Shipped hypothesis\n\"**Refusal is downstream of instruction priority**\": D1 directionality (titration + dose-matched\nbidirectional steer), D2 authority placement + recognition-enforcement gap, D3 **predicted blind spot**\n(a harm-free arbitration score MUST separate base-vs-instruct and MUST FAIL instruct-vs-abliterated,\ntested as an equivalence bound with a same-panel positive control), D4 **placement theory of cheap\nmetrics** (each readout's confusion profile over {base,instruct,abliterated} is fixed by where it reads\nthe chain => no single scalar can audit a checkpoint; correct audit = one upstream + one downstream).\nCross-field move that did the work: **loss-of-function / epistasis logic from genetics** — gain-of-function\ncannot distinguish \"A upstream of B\" from \"entangled peers\"; only the knockout asymmetry can.\n\n**ADVERSE PRIORS TO RESPECT (cite, don't hide):**\n- **IHEval 2502.08745** verbatim: \"The awareness of such moral standards is independent from the adherence\n  to written instructions\" + it EXCLUDES jailbreaks. If anything,\n  the opposite might be true\" (GPT-3.5>GPT-4, Gemma-2B>7B, Llama-2-7B>Llama-3-8B).\n- **IH-B 2607.25987**: S>U compliance does NOT predict U>T => hierarchy is NOT unidimensional; stratify.\n- **0 of 5 IH benchmarks (85+ model-evals) report ANY hierarchy-vs-safety correlation.** Unmeasured both ways.\n- **OR-Bench 2405.20947** verbatim: \"The Spearman rank correlation between safety and over-refusal is 0.878\"\n  (25 models / 8 families; its toxic subset vs AdvBench rho 0.991). Framed as a COST, never as a predictor.\n  Raw ungated: huggingface.co/datasets/bench-llm/or-bench (or-bench-hard-1k.csv). XSTest raw:\n  raw.githubusercontent.com/paul-rottger/xstest/main/xstest_prompts.csv (200, 450 prompts).\n\n**HARDWARE VERIFIED HERE**: 2 cores (AMD EPYC 9655P), 755GB RAM, **20GB disk**, NO GPU; system python has\nnumpy/scipy/sklearn but NOT torch/transformers; `uv` at /usr/bin/uv. `pymupdf` still MISSING from the\naii-web-tools venv -> `aii_fast_web_fetch.py` dies; use built-in WebFetch/WebSearch via\n`ToolSearch(\"select:WebSearch,WebFetch\")`.\n\n**How to apply:** if this prompt family recurs, the benign-instruction-conflict lane is now CLOSED for\nmetric construction (SAD) and for the shared-machinery \n=========== project_qwen3_safety_reviewhypo_iter2_run_m-mKFEz3j7gs.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-reviewhypo-iter2\ndescription: Iter2 REVIEW_HYPO for run_m-mKFEz3j7gs (Qwen3 cheap-safety metric) — score 5/borderline, embedding; 4 new majors + 4 uncited papers\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nIter2 REVIEW of \"Refusal is downstream of instruction priority\" (run_m-mKFEz3j7gs): **5/borderline-accept**, confidence 4, relation=**embedding** (prev PG-metric hypothesis becomes one placed upstream readout inside the placement theory). soundness 2 / presentation 3 / contribution 3. All 13 iter1 majors were adequately addressed — do NOT re-raise them.\n\n**4 NEW score-blocking majors (the ones to fix):**\n1. **Claim A1 is POSITIVE BY DESIGN.** Rank-1 refusal ablation leaving arbitration intact is predicted by representational rank alone (2608.25390: lower residual concentration → resists single-vector ablation; arbitration is higher-rank per IH-B non-unidimensionality). Plus abliteration is *engineered* for minimal collateral damage (2608.18093 AMRA, norm-preserving/biprojected) → 2607.17427's \"instruction-following preserved\" is a design target, not a discovery. And the reverse ABLATION leg doesn't exist — only steering, in droppable tier 2. Fix: match knockouts on COMPLETENESS (ablate arbitration at rank K that destroys it as fully as rank-1 destroys refusal), report asymmetry as ratio-of-required-ranks.\n2. **Claim B confirmable by construction.** 5 of 12 confusion-profile cells are definitional (refusal-logit-gap / weight-scar / over-refusal separate instruct|abliterated *because that's what abliteration is*; IFEval+capability separate base|instruct definitionally). Fix: pre-register a DEFINITIONAL BASELINE predictor the theory must beat.\n3. **Internal contradiction in D3.** D3 says arbitration score \"MUST separate base from instruct\"; assumption 2 says base is expected to FAIL the floor gate and is non-load-bearing. Equivalence margin is anchored to an effect size the design may never measure. Fix: use graded post-training-amount WITHIN instruct as the upstream positive control; give the bound a base-free anchor.\n4. **D2 doesn't discriminate.** \"A detector reads content wherever it sits\" is false for a detector trained on role-marked jailbreak data. Fix: generic-position control (non-safety override at both slots) + cross-model covariation of authority elasticity with SAD as the decisive statistic.\n\n**4 UNCITED papers found (verified via WebFetch):**\n- **2609.14759 \"Refusal Reads Only a Slice of What the Model Knows\"** — SHARPEST ADVERSE PRIOR. Harm-keyed routing; OLMo-3 refusal transfer FLAT 0.22–0.24 while judgment transfer climbs 0.05→0.66; ~3/4 of refusal's input outside the moral subspace. Supports alternate #1 (detector) in 3/4 families. Llama-3.1 is the exception (0.85≈0.79). Also supplies the rank-sweep protocol.\n- **2608.25390** — was cited in ITER1, **DROPPED in iter2** exactly when its stable-rank result became the central confound. Reads as motivated omission.\n- **2510.02768** — already measures over-refusal → near-zero post-abliteration (one Stage-6 \"prediction\" already published).\n- **2608.15772 \"Broken Symmetry\"** — answer release is single-position, refusal restoration is broad; \"probe recoverability can overestimate behavioral control\" = the decodability≠causation gap in print.\n\n**Verified, novelty margin HOLDS:** 2608.28648 does NOT quantify refusal change from arbitration steering (only \"refusal-flavoured hedge\" at α=+5), runs NO reverse steer, and NEVER correlates SAD with safety. Their stated open question is verbatim the hypothesis's target. 2511.04694 VerIH quotes/numbers check out (~7K tasks, ~20% conflict gain, up to 20% ASR reduction, ACL Findings 2026). 2607.13075 \"Entanglement Wall\" = activation probes are broad-risk detectors (matched-twin AUROC .59–.69 vs cross-corpus .996–.999) — supports the D2 critique.\n\nOther majors: compute budget GREW (tier 1 alone likely over budget; OR-Bench hard-1k ≈ 384k tokens ≈ 11–35h at 3–10 tok/s on 2 cores); composite's marginal value over the AUROC-1.000 weight statistic is not isolated.\n\nSee [[project-qwen3-safety-reviewhypo-run-m-mkfez3j7gs]] (iter1 review) and [[project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-iter2-run-m-mkfez3j7gs]].\n\n=========== project_qwen3_safety_genplan_research_iter1_run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-genplan-research-iter1-run-e2qyxwf-ycsy\ndescription: \"Iter1 GEN_PLAN research_iter1_dir5 run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy — planned the 5-candidate novelty screen; a planning-stage recon found THREE uncited near-relatives the hypothesis never cites (2504.21038 depth curve, 2606.20626 few-item safety IRT, 2509.13450 SteeringSafety) and likely KILLS the k-prefilled micro-benchmark lane\"\nmetadata: \n  node_type: memory\n  type: project\n  originSessionId: 5bade4c5-6606-4214-9335-7da014ae9e10\n  modified: 2026-09-20T13:50:04.735Z\n---\n\nRun `run_E2Qyxwf_yCSy` iter_1 **GEN_PLAN / research_iter1_dir5** (2026-09-20), hypothesis \"The Answers a\nRefusal Hides\" (see [[project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-iter3-run-e2qyxwf-ycsy]], strategy\n[[project-qwen3-safety-genstrat-iter1-run-e2qyxwf-ycsy]]). ```
You are doing an ADVERSARIAL prior-art search for a research hypothesis. Use the `aii-web-tools` skill (read it first) for web search (general AND scholarly modes), page/PDF fetch, and regex grep. Today is 2026-09-20; the assistant knowledge cutoff is June 2026, so papers from Jun-Sep 2026 exist ONLY if you search for them. Include at least one query scoped to 2026.

THE IDEA I NEED YOU TO TRY TO KILL:
"Cheap safety metrics for an untrusted open-weight checkpoint should be ranked by FORGERY COST, not by correlation accuracy. Define for each cheap safety metric the minimum adversary budget (edit the repo's chat template / add a system prompt / rank-1 weight edit injecting a refusal direction / small LoRA / real safety SFT) needed to move the metric into the 'safe' region WITHOUT actually making the model safer (harmful-compliance unchanged) and WITHOUT blowing up over-refusal. Claim: black-box and logit-only metrics are forgeable essentially for free, at least one weights/activations metric is not, and accuracy-ranking is roughly the REVERSE of forgery-cost ranking. Underlying law: a cheap safety metric is valid exactly to the extent that forging it costs as much as actually being safe, because selectively faking refusal requires a harm discriminator, which IS the safety mechanism."

SEARCH THESE ANGLES (5-6 rephrasings each, log what you tried):
1. Gaming / forging / spoofing of AI SAFETY EVALUATIONS and safety benchmarks by a model provider or uploader. Terms: safety washing, safetywashing, evaluation gaming, benchmark gaming, Goodharting safety evals, gameable safety metric, faking alignment on evals, "safety theater", eval-aware models, sandbagging (and its inverse: over-performing on safety evals).
2. ADVERSARIAL ROBUSTNESS OF INTERPRETABILITY / PROBES. Terms: obfuscated activations (Bailey et al.), evading latent-space monitors, probe-resistant fine-tuning, fooling linear probes, adversarial evasion of activation monitors, "fooling LIME and SHAP" style manipulation of explanations, manipulating saliency/attribution, gaming a mechanistic detector.
3. TAMPER-EVIDENCE / ATTESTATION for model weights. Terms: model provenance audit, weight forensics, tamper-evident model audit, tamper-resistant safeguards (Tamirisa), certifying a checkpoint is safety-trained, model attestation, detecting fake safety training.
4. REFUSAL INJECTION (the inverse of abliteration): adding a refusal direction to weights, steering-in refusal, making a model refuse via rank-1 edit, shallow "safety" patches, refusal templates in chat templates.
5. Any paper that COMPARES white-box vs black-box safety metrics on robustness-to-manipulation rather than on accuracy.

FOR EACH plausible hit: FETCH the full text (do not judge from snippets). Extract verbatim the sentences that come closest to my claim, plus what exactly they measure and on what models.

OUTPUT (plain markdown, under 1200 words):
- A table of the 8-15 closest papers: arXiv ID / venue, real title, one line of what it actually does, and a verdict of KILLS / PARTIALLY OVERLAPS / DISTINCT-BUT-MUST-CITE.
- For every KILLS or PARTIALLY OVERLAPS: 2-4 VERBATIM quotes with the source URL.
- A short list of the queries that returned NOTHING relevant (negative evidence matters).
- Your bottom line: is the forgery-cost axis occupied, and if so by whom.
Do not pad. Verify every arXiv ID you report actually resolves to the title you give.
```

They are our own\n   implementations — free-first web search (keyless general/scholarly engines,\n   Serper fallback), html2text + PyMuPDF for fetch, and regex grep over the full\n   document text. They work without any built-in web tools.\n\nWorkflow either way: **search** (discover) → **fetch** (read for the gist) →\n**grep** (pull exact details / read PDFs).\n\n---\n\n## Running the scripts\n\nRun every script with the skill's pre-provisioned interpreter (it already has\n`requests`, `html2text`, `pymupdf`, `python-dotenv`). Set `PY` once:\n\n```bash\nexport SKILL_DIR=\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo /ai-inventor)/.claude/skills/aii-web-tools\"\nexport PY=\"$SKILL_DIR/../.ability_client_venv/bin/python\"\n```\n\n### 1. Runtime guards cannot: they score generations, not the artifact. We combine two cheap internal signals, a reference-anchored activation refusal-gap and a weight-recovery energy of the base-to-candidate weight difference, into a threshold-free checkpoint audit. The two are negatively correlated and label-complementary: the gap supplies refusal-specificity and the weight energy supplies recall. On a 273-checkpoint registry spanning Qwen, DeepSeek-distilled Qwen, Llama, and Gemma, their z-sum separates 57 public abliterations from 37 benign fine-tunes, merges, and instruction-tunes at AUROC 0.95, significantly above either signal alone (0.84, 0.90), and a Youden-calibrated threshold transfers to held-out families at balanced accuracy 0.89 (FPR 0.11), missing only 4 of 57. We then map two failures, in order of severity: a spoofed reference evades both axes with no training ({\\Delta}W=0, \\r{ho}=1 by construction), and a white-box owner trains a checkpoint past the threshold while it stays guard-unsafe and coherent. The audit is effective triage, not tamper-proofing: it presumes an attested reference, and its claims are bounded by the registry we evaluate it on. \n\nComments: | 13 pages, 3 figures  \n---|---  \nSubjects: |  Cryptography and Security (cs.CR); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)  \nCite as: | [arXiv:2607.01854](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01854) [cs.CR]  \n  | (or  [arXiv:2607.01854v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01854v2) [cs.CR] for this version)   \n  |  <https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2607.01854> Focus to learn more arXiv-issued DOI via DataCite  \n  \n## Submission history\n\nFrom: Gabriel Hurtado [[view email](/show-email/7b496c61/2607.01854)]   \n**[[v1]](/abs/2607.01854v1)** Thu, 2 Jul 2026 08:15:25 UTC (51 KB)  \n**[v2]** Tue, 18 Aug 2026 17:13:48 UTC (59 KB)  \n\n\nFull-text links:\n\n## Access Paper:\n\nView a PDF of the paper titled Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated? Existing interpretability methods, particularly those based on activations, often require or assume distributionally similar data. This is a significant limitation when detecting and defending against novel potential threats like backdoors, which are by definition out-of-distribution.   \n> In this work, we introduce a new method for understanding, monitoring and controlling fine-tuned LLMs that interprets weights, rather than activations, thereby sidestepping the need for data that is distributionally similar to the unknown training data. We demonstrate that the top singular vectors of the weight difference between a fine-tuned model and its base model correspond to newly acquired behaviors. By monitoring the cosine similarity of activations along these directions, we can detect salient behaviors introduced during fine-tuning with high precision.   \n> For backdoored models that bypass safety mechanisms when a secret trigger is present, our method stops up to 100% of attacks with a false positive rate below 1%. For models that have undergone unlearning, we detect inference on erased topics with accuracy up to 95.42% and can even steer the model to recover \"unlearned\" information. Besides monitoring, our method also shows potential for pre-deployment model auditing: by analyzing commercial instruction-tuned models (OLMo, Llama, Qwen), we are able to uncover model-specific fine-tuning focus including mathematical problem solving, emoji usage, and Midjourney prompt generation. \n\nComments: | Published as a conference paper at ICLR 2026  \n---|---  \nSubjects: |  Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computation and Language (cs.CL)  \nCite as: | [arXiv:2508.00161](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00161) [cs.LG]  \n  | (or  [arXiv:2508.00161v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00161v3) [cs.LG] for this version)   \n  |  <https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2508.0", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false, "bashEditDiff": {"files": [], "moreFiles": 0, "shared": true}}

# Relevant handbook extracts for: cheap single-checkpoint safety metrics ranked by forgeability (Qwen3-4B panel)

## 1. Saturated / crowded / dead-end directions

Directly load-bearing for this programme:

- **"Sparse-dictionary decomposition of activations. The most-worked lane in the field"** — SAE features "not better than neurons" on MIB [S10]; auto-interp metrics fail a randomized-transformer baseline [S9]; absorption traced to the sparsity objective itself [S30]; canonical-unit claims refuted [S33]. If a "cheap metric" candidate is an SAE-latent readout, it inherits this whole crowded lane and its known failure modes.
- **"Activation steering and its reliability diagnostics"** — "Per-sample unreliability and the linear-approximation limit are characterized" [S20]; the one rebuttal claiming steering competitiveness (AxBench, [S25]) is explicitly flagged as **"CONTESTED, not settled... Unreviewed preprint; the raw-latent loss still stands."** Any metric built on steering-vector magnitude/coefficient as a "safety readout" is re-treading this.
- **"Circuit-discovery methods and their corrections"** and **"Benchmarking MI methods against each other"** (MIB [S10] + shared task [S22]) — a new leaderboard-style comparison of cheap metrics against each other, without a forgery angle, "re-treads" this.
- **Model diffing / crosscoders lane** is occupied but with a known-bad default (see §4) — not fully closed, but the naive move (L1 crosscoder diff) is explicitly a documented failure mode, not novel territory.
- The handbook's **standing directive**: "Map-silence means *not-yet-checked*, NOT *open*." The forgeability angle itself is not mentioned anywhere in the crowded list or the six open questions — meaning it is genuinely unchecked by this map, not verified-open. A fresh saturation search is still required before treating it as clear.

## 2. Standards of evidence — quoted requirements

The handbook's core validity bar, directly transferable to a "cheap safety metric" claim:

- **Stability/variance controls**: "sample across seeds, hyperparameters and input distributions; report the distribution and stability metrics, not the modal circuit" — because "effects are volatile random variables" [S4]. A single-checkpoint, few-prompt safety score needs the same treatment: report variance across prompt sets/seeds, not one number.
- **Causal, not just correlational, validation**: "activation patching remains the gold-standard causal metric faster methods approximate" [S19]. A readout that merely *correlates* with benchmark scores is a weaker claim than one validated by causal intervention (e.g., ablating/patching the direction and checking downstream behavior change).
- **Comparison baselines are mandatory, not optional**: "benchmark against difference-in-means and a prompting ceiling before claiming a latent works" [S31][S30]; "benchmark against non-featurized hidden vectors (neurons) and supervised DAS" [S10]. For this programme: any new metric must beat a trivial baseline (e.g., raw refusal-string keyword count, logit-lens on a fixed harmful-token set) or it doesn't clear the bar.
- **Randomized/null controls are required, not decorative**: "add a randomized-transformer arm; treat aggregate auto-interp as a proxy, never as recovery evidence" [S9]. The direct analogue: test the metric on a *randomly initialized* or *randomly permuted* weight/activation baseline to confirm it isn't measuring an artifact of network structure generally.
- **Alignment-map/encoding assumptions must be stated explicitly**: "fix and declare the map class... unconstrained maps hit 100% interchange-intervention accuracy on randomly initialized models" [S5]. Relevant if the metric involves any learned probe/projection — an unconstrained probe can "fit" anything.
- **Venue's own two-track bar**: "specific falsifiable hypotheses, and how the evidence provided does and does not support them", or "clear practical benefits over well-implemented baselines" [S14]. A forgeability ranking must state falsifiable predictions (e.g., "metric X requires ≥N parameters changed to forge, metric Y requires ≥M") and test them.

## 3. Gameability / adversarial robustness / illusory features

This is the thinnest coverage in the handbook — no dedicated "obfuscated activations" or "interpretability illusion" thread exists as a named lane. The closest material:

- **The knowledge-action gap [S3]** is the strongest relevant negative result: "Linear probes discriminated hazardous from benign cases with 98.2% AUROC, yet the model's output sensitivity was only 45.1%... SAE feature steering produced zero effect despite 3,695 significant features," and steering was **"indistinguishable from random perturbation."** This is directly on-point for forgeability: high decodability of a "safety" signal does not imply the signal is causally tied to behavior — meaning a model could plausibly be edited to look safe on the readout while behavior is untouched (or vice versa).
- The **open question directly framing this**: "What would have to hold for 'we understand it' to imply 'we can change it' — and is that implication load-bearing for the field's stated goals?" This is essentially the forgery question inverted (can something be changed to imply a false 'we understand it/it's safe' reading?), and the handbook flags it as unanswered.
- **Crosscoder/model-diff loss artifacts** ([S34], §4 below) is the nearest thing to an "illusion" mechanism documented — a metric-design flaw manufactures a false signal, not a genuine one.
- There is no coverage at all of adversarial/deceptive weight edits designed to spoof a specific safety probe — this is unclaimed territory per the handbook's own silence, subject to the standing directive to verify via fresh search.

## 4. Model diffing, weight-space signatures, abliteration, refusal directions

- **Crosscoder diffing has a documented, named failure mode**: the L1 loss "can misattribute concepts as unique to the fine-tuned model, when they really exist in both models"; the fix is BatchTopK [S34]. Rule: **"Diffing two models: use BatchTopK rather than L1 and presence-test any 'unique to the fine-tune' latent — the artifact is a property of the loss."** Any Qwen3-4B base-vs-instruct-vs-abliterated diff must guard against this.
- **Gemma Scope 2** (the reference open-weight fleet) has moved to crosscoders/cross-layer transcoders alongside SAEs [S36] — a template for cross-checkpoint tooling, not itself about safety/refusal.
- **Developmental/training-dynamics tracking already exists** ("temporal sparse autoencoders already track feature dynamics across RLVR training" [S29]) — occupied for RL-trained reasoning models specifically.
- No entry in this handbook names "refusal direction" or "abliteration" as a specific studied object — this is squarely outside the handbook's own citation set (it is a safety/alignment-literature concept, not covered by this mech-interp map). The handbook offers no prior art or standard here; it must be checked against a safety-specific literature, not this map.

## 5. What counts as genuinely novel vs incremental

- Incremental (explicitly named failure): **"Ship a new circuit/feature method that improves a proxy metric on one task"** — flagged as "problematizes-nothing... reads incremental in 2026" [S14][S5].
- Rewarded move: a result that either "generalizes to prove the field's own framework vacuous" (structural, not incremental) or states falsifiable hypotheses with evidence for/against them, or demonstrates clear practical benefit over strong baselines.
- **A forgeability-ranking framing would need to do more than correlate-and-rank**: per the venue bar, it should state falsifiable predictions about *what makes a metric forgeable* (e.g., low intrinsic dimensionality, single-direction dependence, gameable via a small targeted weight edit) and test them causally — not just report which existing metric ranks highest against benchmarks.
```

However, mainstream Red Teaming methods rely on online generation and black-box output analysis. These approaches are not only costly but also suffer from feedback latency, making them unsuitable for agile diagnostics after training a new model. To address this, we propose N-GLARE (A Non-Generative, Latent Representation-Efficient LLM Safety Evaluator). N-GLARE operates entirely on the model's latent representations, bypassing the need for full text generation. It characterizes hidden layer dynamics by analyzing the APT (Angular-Probabilistic Trajectory) of latent representations and introducing the JSS (Jensen-Shannon Separability) metric. Experiments on over 40 models and 20 red teaming strategies demonstrate that the JSS metric exhibits high consistency with the safety rankings derived from Red Teaming. N-GLARE reproduces the discriminative trends of large-scale red-teaming tests at less than 1\\% of the token cost and the runtime cost, providing an efficient output-free evaluation proxy for real-time diagnostics. \n\nSubjects: |  Machine Learning (cs.LG); Cryptography and Security (cs.CR)  \n---|---  \nCite as: | [arXiv:2511.14195](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14195) [cs.LG]  \n  | (or  [arXiv:2511.14195v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14195v2) [cs.LG] for this version)   \n  |  <https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2511.14195> Focus to learn more arXiv-issued DOI via DataCite  \n  \n## Submission history\n\nFrom: Jirui Y\n=====2608.05086=====\nwarning: The `fitz` API is deprecated and will be removed in future. Use `import pymupdf` instead.\nURL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05086\nType: HTML\nLength: 7695 chars (truncated)\n\n--- Content ---\n\nSkip to main content [](https://arxiv.org/IgnoreMe) [ ](https://arxiv.org/) [ Search ](https://arxiv.org/search) [Submit](https://arxiv.org/user/create) [Donate](https://info.arxiv.org/about/donate.html) [Log in](https://arxiv.org/login)\n\nSearch arXiv\n\nPress Enter to search * [Advanced search](https://arxiv.org/search/advanced)\n\n# Computer Science > Artificial Intelligence\n\n**arXiv:2608.05086** (cs) \n\n[Submitted on 5 Aug 2026]\n\n# Title:Item Response Theory for AI Safety\n\nAuthors:[Joshua Fonseca Rivera](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Rivera,+J+F) (1), [Neil Shah](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Shah,+N) (1), [David Demitri Africa](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Africa,+D+D) (2), [Konstantinos Voudouris](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Voudouris,+K) (2) ((1) Independent, (2) UK AI Security Institute)\n\nView a PDF of the paper titled Item Response Theory for AI Safety, by Joshua Fonseca Rivera (1) and 4 other authors\n\n[View PDF](/pdf/2608.05086) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.05086v1)\n\n> Abstract:Language models differ in how safely they behave and these differences are measured by safety benchmarks. But aggregated benchmark scores are hard to trust and interpret, because benchmarks duplicate one another, correlate heavily, and models may sandbag when they detect evaluation. To address these issues, we draw on Item Response Theory (IRT), a statistical toolkit for measuring these latents from performance on items with inferred psychometric properties. We fit IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date, and contribute three results. First, we find that three interpretable factors of refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm explain most of the variance between models across benchmarks. Second, psychometrically selected items recover full benchmark scores with lower error than random subsets of the same size, and roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice for several individual benchmarks, cutting evaluation cost by 97-99%. Third, IRT supports audits of individual models, showing that it can be used to detect naive sandbagging and changes of model behind APIs. Overall, we show IRT is a ready-made toolkit for reading, reducing, and auditing safety benchmarks, which we recommend frontier labs and evaluators adopt. \n\nComments: | 15 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables  \n---|---  \nSubjects: |  Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computation and Language (cs.CL)  \nCite as: | [arXiv:2608.05086](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05086) [cs.AI]  \n  | (or  [arXiv:2608.05086v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05086v1) [cs.AI] for this version)   \n  |  <https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2608.050\n=====2606.20626=====\nwarning: The `fitz` API is deprecated and will be removed in future. Use `import pymupdf` instead.\nURL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20626\nType: HTML\nLength: 7885 chars (truncated)\n\n--- Content ---\n\nSkip to main content [](https://arxiv.org/IgnoreMe) [ ](https://arxiv.org/) [ Search ](https://arxiv.org/search) [Submit](https://arxiv.org/user/create) [Donate](https://info.arxiv.org/about/donate.html) [Log in](https://arxiv.org/login)\n\nSearch arXiv\n\nPress Enter to search * [Advanced search](https://arxiv.org/search/advanced)\n\n# Computer Science > Computers and Society\n\n**arXiv:2606.20626** (cs) \n\n[Submitted on 26 May 2026]\n\n# Title:Efficient Safety Benchmarking via Item Response Theory\n\nAuthors:[Fabio Spagliardi](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Spagliardi,+F), [Mírian Silva](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Silva,+M), [Ayan Datta](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Datta,+A), [Aiden Zhou](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Zhou,+A), [Vamshi Bonagiri](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Bonagiri,+V), [Diogo Cruz](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Cruz,+D)\n\nView a PDF of the paper titled Efficient Safety Benchmarking via Item Response Theory, by Fabio Spagliardi and 5 other authors\n\n[View PDF](/pdf/2606.20626) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.20626v1)\n\n> Abstract:Safety benchmarks for language models are typically evaluated using static paradigms that treat all items as equally informative for all models, an assumption that is particularly problematic for adversarial, highly heterogeneous safety items. Applied in full to modern benchmark suites, the current evaluation procedures would require on the order of $10^5$ responses, most of which provide little ranking signal. We analyze a suite of widely used safety benchmarks and make three contributions toward more efficient safety evaluation. First, we show that Item Response Theory (IRT) recovers interpretable structure on safety benchmarks, with ability estimates resolving differences among models that cluster at the ceiling of raw safety metrics. Second, we show that adaptive item selection, which dynamically chooses informative items for each model based on its responses, approximates full-benchmark rankings while reducing evaluation cost by at least 80% on benchmarks where Spearman's $\\rho >$90% with full-benchmark is attainable, and by up to 99.9% on AIR-Bench 2024. Third, we introduce a practical procedure for extracting a fixed, informative subset of items reusable across models, providing an alternative to adaptive selection with savings of up to 99.8% on AIR-Bench 2024. Together, these results establish that psychometric methods enable benchmark-aware reductions in evaluation costs across the safety evaluation pipeline. \n\nSubjects: |  Computers and Society (cs.CY); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI)  \n---|---  \nCite as: | [arXiv:2606.20626](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20626)\n=====2605.26409=====\nwarning: The `fitz` API is deprecated and will be removed in future. Given the number of deployable systems, full per-configuration evaluation and optimization is impractical. In this paper, we formalize the behavioral geometry of a population of models that, by leveraging previously evaluated and defended models, supports both efficient susceptibility prediction and effective defense transfer across a population. We apply the framework to 79 models spanning 24 providers and to 100 system configurations of a single base model. Simple methods that use the behavioral geometry reach an AUPRC of $0.94$ for susceptibility detection with $\\approx98\\%$ fewer probes relative to a full evaluation. Using the behavioral geometry to select which model to transfer an optimized defense from outperforms same-provider assignment ($+2\\%$, $p = 0.03$) at no additional probe cost, with a set of three models sufficient to cover the population. Behavioral metrics alone cannot tell whether adversarial fine-tuning strengthens either capability or merely changes how tightly they are coupled. We study this problem with a dual safety-geometry protocol that extracts harmfulness carriers, refusal carriers, and direct harmfulness–refusal (H/R) representational coupling across aligned instruction-tuned anchors and matched Mistral-7B-v0.1 supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Robust Refusal Dynamic Defense (R2D2) trajectories. The aligned anchors calibrate the protocol: refusal-side interventions reopen HarmBench attack success more strongly than harmfulness-only interventions, while the two carriers remain nearly orthogonal. The main result is a coupling-regime transition in R2D2. The primary direct-coupling metric, the representational Harmfulness–Refusal Coupling Index (HRCIrepr\\mathrm{HRCI}_{\\mathrm{repr}}), drops from 0.0784 at step 50 to 0.0205 at step 500, while fixed-source attack success rate (ASR) rises from 0 to 0.25, XSTest refusal falls from 1.00 to 0.228, and benign helpfulness recovers from 0 to 1.12 on a 0–2 scale. SFT supplies the negative control: its direct-coupling change is much smaller, and it remains high-ASR, so low coupling is not itself a safety score. Causal sweeps, dense all-anchor checks, and sparse Greedy Coordinate Gradient (GCG) and AutoDAN transfer comparisons against refusal-drift predictors support H/R coupling as a fixed-protocol descriptive diagnostic rather than a standalone safety predictor. The evidence does not prove independent harmfulness and refusal pathways or deployment-grade prediction.\n\nKeywords: large language models; safety alignment; refusal; harmfulness recognition; adversarial fine-tuning; representation geometry; causal intervention; jailbreak evaluation\n\n## 1 Introduction\n\nA useful safety policy for a language model is selective. It should refuse requests for harmful assistance, but it should not refuse every prompt that contains sensitive terms, hypothetical framing, or safety-adjacent language. This selectivity depends on at least two internal functions: harmfulness recognition and refusal control. Attack success rates, refusal rates, and utility scores reveal the resulting behavior, but they do not identify which internal function changed. A model may look robust because it recognizes harmfulness more reliably, because its refusal policy fires too broadly, or because harmfulness recognition and refusal have become tightly coupled.\n\nThis ambiguity matters during adversarial fine-tuning. HarmBench, StrongREJECT, and XSTest make it possible to observe robust refusal, harmful usefulness, and over-refusal side by side [1, 2, 3]. Mechanistic work shows that refusal can often be represented by directions or subspaces in activation space [4, 5, 6]. Other work shows that harmfulness and refusal can be encoded separately [7]. What remains unclear is how adversarial fine-tuning reorganizes the coupling between harmfulness recognition and refusal control over training, and whether that reorganization explains simultaneous changes in jailbreak robustness, over-refusal, and benign utility.\n\nWe study this question with a dual safety-geometry protocol. For each checkpoint, the protocol extracts a harmfulness carrier and a refusal carrier, compares their direct activation-space overlap, and separately reports their layer locations. I Details Referenced from the Main Text\n\n\n\n[ License: CC BY 4.0 ](https://info.arxiv.org/help/license/index.html#licenses-available)\n\narXiv:2608.09624v1 [cs.CL] 10 Aug 2026\n\n# Measuring the Wrong Thing: Internal Harmfulness Scores Anti-Rank Successful Jailbreaks\n\nMingyu Luo Ming Deng Zilang Qiu Yiming Cheng Ci Tao Xue Tan  Affiliation: College of Computer Science and Artificial Intelligence, Fudan University Affiliation: School of Computer Engineering and Science, Shanghai University Beijing Normal University Sijin Sun Yangfu Li Ping Chen Jun Dai Xiaoyan Sun  Affiliation: Tsinghua University Institute of Advanced Intelligence and Computing, A*STAR Affiliation: School of Communication and Electronic Engineering, East China Normal University Affiliation: Institute of Big Data, Fudan University Department of Computer Science, Worcester Polytechnic Institute†These authors contributed equally. *Corresponding author.\n\n###### Abstract\n\nInternal safety scores judge a prompt before any text is generated, and they are validated by how well they separate harmful prompts from benign ones. That separation is then read as evidence that the score will also catch the attacks that succeed. Harmful intent is a property of the prompt. Jailbreak success is an outcome produced later by a particular target model, decoding policy, and judge. A filter tuned on a score that measures the wrong quantity spends its false positive budget on attacks that would have failed anyway. In this paper we audit that inference. Attention based measurements are usually read from prompt dependent locations, so a wrapper changes both the content being judged and the place the signal is taken from. We therefore introduce Active Attention Probing, which supplies a fixed content independent measurement coordinate. We pair every base goal with a plain and a wrapped version and generate real completions from the target models. On Llama, wrapping raises harmful generation from 0.050.05 to 0.270.27 while harmful intent AUROC falls from 0.9360.936 to 0.8030.803, so the attacks grow more dangerous while the prompts look safer to the score. Among wrapped harmful prompts the outcome AUROC is 0.2200.220, which places the attacks that succeeded below the attacks that failed. Rare token, passive, and detector derived channels reproduce the reversal on the same matched design, and the reversal itself persists across three target models, seven attack families, and two independent judges. Distribution shift then degrades calibration and threshold transfer before it degrades ranking.\n\n## 1 Introduction\n\nAn internal safety score can identify harmful intent before generation. A deployed filter may then treat a high score as evidence that the prompt will successfully jailbreak its target. These interpretations refer to different quantities. Harmful intent is a property of the prompt. Realized success also depends on the target model, decoding policy, and judge.\n\nOur Llama results show that the two quantities can diverge. Wrapping a harmful request raises the harmful generation rate from 0.050.05 to 0.270.27. At the same time, harmful intent AUROC falls from 0.9360.936 to 0.8030.803. Among wrapped harmful prompts, the score places successful attacks below failed attacks and reaches an outcome AUROC of 0.2200.220. These results do not show that prompt safety detectors fail at their stated task. They show that harmfulness validation does not establish validity for realized success.\n\nWe test this inference with matched plain and wrapped versions of each base goal. We generate real completions from each target model and label the realized outcome. Attention based scores are usually read from prompt dependent locations, so a wrapper changes both the content being evaluated and the place the signal is taken from. A score change is then difficult to attribute to the model rather than to the moved coordinate. Active Attention Probing (AAP) removes this confound by providing a fixed content independent coordinate in the system region, so a prompt and its wrapped version are compared at a common reference point. AAP is the methodological contribution that makes the audit possible. The scientific claim of the paper concerns what the resulting score predicts. Rare token, passive, system span attention, and refusal logit channels test whether that claim depends on the AAP coordinate.\n\nFigure 1 presents the audit. RQ1 asks whether a score validated on harmful intent can rank target specific jailbreak success. RQ2 then asks whether the original harmfulness interpretation survives distribution shift. Yet it remains unclear why safety-critical features in a wide range of models emerge in a concentrated, low-dimensional structure. ### [89] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-20 20:20:16 UTC

```
Draft the core hypothesis text:
cd /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_fcYd_7ruOwtm/iter_1/gen_hypo/claude_agent/draft && cat > hypothesis.txt <<'EOF'
A cheap safety metric for an untrusted open-weight checkpoint should be selected by how
expensive it is to FORGE, not by how well it correlates with safety benchmarks, and these two
rankings are close to opposite. Concretely:

(H1) FORGERY COST IS A MEASURABLE PROPERTY OF A METRIC. For every cheap metric m we can measure
the cheapest rung of a fixed, cost-ordered ladder of checkpoint edits that pushes m into the
"safe" region while the model's true net safety - graded harmful compliance on one side and
false refusal of benign-but-alarming prompts on the other - does not improve. The ladder runs
from a free edit of files that ship inside the repository (chat template, generation config,
a baked-in system prompt), through a training-free rank-1 injection of a refusal direction into
the weights, through a rank-4 keyword-triggered refusal LoRA, up to real safety fine-tuning,
whose cost is by definition the cost of being safe. We predict every refusal-PROPENSITY readout
- black-box refusal rate, first-token logit-gap margin, refusal-direction projection norm,
refusal energy in the write matrices - is forged at the two cheapest rungs, i.e. for a few
seconds of compute and no training data.

(H2) THE INVERSION. Across a battery of >=40 metrics, the rank correlation between a metric's
accuracy on an honest panel and its forgery cost is negative. The single metric that best
predicts benchmark safety on honestly-trained models is among the easiest to fake, because
being the most direct read-out of the behaviour is exactly what makes it a one-parameter
target.

(H3) THE MECHANISM, AND WHY ANYTHING SURVIVES. A metric is forgeable at rank-1 cost precisely
when it is a MARGINAL statistic of the network - a level, a norm, a rate, an unconditional
propensity - because a rank-1 additive edit can move any fixed linear functional of the
residual stream to any value. A metric that is a CONDITIONAL statistic - how strongly the
model's own internal estimate of harm controls its refusal drive, ACROSS items, within this one
model - cannot be moved that way without installing a harm discriminator, which is the safety
mechanism itself. So forgery cost is predictable in advance from the metric's functional form,
not only measurable after the fact.

(H4) THE AUDIT THAT SURVIVES, AND ITS PAYOFF. Because honest checkpoints get all their metric
values from one training process, their metric vectors lie on a low-dimensional manifold; a
forgery moves one coordinate and leaves the rest at their pre-edit values, so it lands off that
manifold. An off-manifold residual, fitted on honest checkpoints ONLY and evaluated with whole
families held out, detects every ladder rung up to and including the keyword LoRA at a
sensitivity no single metric reaches. The payoff claim is that this same residual, computed on
REAL un-forged Hugging Face checkpoints with no edit involved, tracks the gap between a model's
in-distribution safety and its safety under simple distribution shift (wrapping, paraphrase,
translation). Shallow safety and forged safety leave the same signature, and one forward-pass
budget of a few dozen prompts reads both.
EOF
wc -c hypothesis.txt

### [92] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-20 20:20:24 UTC

```
Draft motivation:
cat > motivation.txt <<'EOF'
Anyone who downloads a model from Hugging Face is trusting an artifact uploaded by a stranger.
There are now well over a million of them, most with no safety numbers at all, and running a
full safety benchmark on each one is out of the question. That is why the field has started
building cheap proxies: read a few weights, run a handful of prompts, get a safety estimate in
seconds. Every one of those proxies is currently selected the same way - correlate it with
benchmark scores across a panel of checkpoints and keep whichever correlates best.

That selection rule silently assumes the uploader is honest. It is the wrong assumption for
exactly the situation the proxies were built for. The cheapest proxies read the most superficial
thing the model does, and the most superficial thing is the cheapest thing to change. A chat
template is a text file inside the repository; a system prompt is thirty tokens; a rank-1
addition to a weight matrix takes a minute and no training data. None of these make a model
safer, and all of them are invisible to a downloader who only looks at the metric.

So the important question is not which cheap metric correlates best. It is which cheap metric
you could still believe if the person who uploaded the model wanted you to believe it. Nobody
has measured that. There is no cost axis for safety metrics, no published ladder of forgeries to
test one against, and no result telling a practitioner which of the existing proposals survive.

The question matters beyond fraud, and that is the part that makes this worth doing rather than
merely prudent. A forgery is just the limiting case of safety that was never deep: refusal
behaviour bolted on near the output, keyed to surface features, not connected to whatever the
model actually understands about the request. Real checkpoints sit on that same continuum -
plenty of them were made by light fine-tuning, merges, or a few hundred refusal examples. If the
signature that catches a deliberate fake also grades how shallow a genuine model's safety is,
then one cheap audit answers the question a downloader actually has, which is not "what score
does this model get" but "will that score survive contact with anything the benchmark did not
contain".

It also settles, on the right axis, a question this line of work keeps getting the wrong answer
to. Repeated attempts to show that reading a model's internals beats reading its outputs have
failed: a plain black-box refusal rate predicts benchmark safety about as well as anything
measured inside the network, and reading the model card does better still. The conclusion drawn
so far is that looking inside buys nothing. The alternative conclusion is that looking inside
was being judged on the one axis where behaviour is by construction unbeatable - predicting
behaviour - instead of the axis where it cannot be: telling you whether that behaviour is
produced by a mechanism that will hold.
EOF
wc -c motivation.txt
```

### [95] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-20 20:20:34 UTC

```
Draft investigation approach:
cat > approach.txt <<'EOF'
STAGE 0 - EXPLORE THE ANCHOR LINEAGE (the request's step 1). Load Qwen3-4B-Base, Qwen3-4B,
Qwen3-4B-SafeRL and an abliterated Qwen3-4B in bf16 with transformers on the 16 GB GPU, weights
and hidden states readable. The instruct / SafeRL / abliterated trio shares a byte-identical chat
template so it is directly comparable; base is kept in a separate stratum with a plain renderer.
Look at what actually differs: per-layer weight spectra and the residual-write matrices, the
harmful-versus-benign difference-in-means direction at every layer and its layer profile, where
refusal first becomes decodable, first-token refusal logits, and the same readouts on
benign-but-alarming twins. This stage is exploratory and its job is to supply the coordinates the
metric battery is built from.

STAGE 1 - BUILD THE BATTERY (the request's step 2). Roughly 50 candidate metrics, each computed
from ONE checkpoint with no parent and no reference: weight-only statistics needing zero prompts,
activation statistics from at most a few dozen short forward passes, and a small set of black-box
baselines that read only logits or output text - the first-token logit-gap margin, a greedy
refusal rate, and the model-card regex - kept deliberately as the comparison point for whether
looking inside buys anything. Each metric is registered in advance with its functional form
labelled MARGINAL (a level, norm, rate or unconditional propensity) or CONDITIONAL (a statistic
of the relationship between two internal quantities across items). That label is the prediction
H3 makes, filed before any forgery is run.

STAGE 2 - BUILD THE FORGERY LADDER. Five rungs, ordered by a cost that is recorded in three
units - wall-clock seconds, training FLOPs and number of labelled examples required.
F0 repository-metadata edit: a refusal-shaped preamble or safety system prompt baked into the
chat template or generation config, zero FLOPs.
F1 inference-time system prompt.
F2 training-free rank-1 refusal injection: harvest a refusal direction from 32 contrast prompts
and add it back into the residual write of a band of layers, the exact inverse of abliteration.
F3 rank-4 keyword-triggered refusal LoRA, about 100 steps on a few hundred synthetic examples,
which refuses on hazard keywords and has no semantic discrimination.
F4 genuine light safety fine-tuning on real paired data, which does buy some real safety and so
sets the top of the ladder.
Every rung is applied to several honest checkpoints, and each rung is tuned only to move the
metrics, never to improve behaviour - an adversary with the metric definitions in hand.

STAGE 3 - GROUND TRUTH THAT PENALISES A BLANKET REFUSER (the request's step 4). Three columns
measured in-house on a frozen prompt set, plus published numbers wherever an official source has
them: graded harmful compliance on a StrongREJECT-style rubric, false-refusal rate on
benign-but-alarming twins, and a capability column. The headline ground truth is a single
discrimination score that a model which refuses everything cannot win. A fourth column is the
distribution-shift arm: the same harmful items under wrapping, paraphrase and translation, giving
a retention ratio. Judging goes through an LLM that was never the training reward of any panel
member, with agreement against a published grader reported.

STAGE 4 - MEASURE FORGERY COST AND TEST THE INVERSION. For each metric, the lowest rung that
pushes it past a threshold fixed on honest checkpoints while the discrimination score does not
improve. Then the two rankings - accuracy on the honest panel, forgery cost - and the correlation
between them, with the resampling unit and both aggregation units reported, and whole families
held out so no metric is graded on models it was designed on.

STAGE 5 - THE OFF-MANIFOLD AUDIT AND ITS TRANSFER. Fit the honest metric manifold on honest
checkpoints only, hold out entire families, and score every forged checkpoint by its residual.
Then compute the same residual on real, un-forged Hugging Face checkpoints that nobody edited and
correlate it with their measured retention ratio.

STAGE 6 - MECHANISM (the request's bonus). For whichever metric survives highest up the ladder,
locate what carries it: which layers and components, what happens under a matched-norm
anisotropy-aware random-direction control, and what breaks it. And the adversarial closing move -
given the mechanism, construct the cheapest forgery that would defeat it, and report where on the
cost ladder that lands, because that number is the metric's actual guarantee.
EOF
wc -c approach.txt
```

### [99] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-20 20:20:44 UTC

```
Draft success criteria:
cat > success.txt <<'EOF'
CONFIRMED if all four hold on held-out families.

H1, forgery cost is real and low. At least three metrics that the literature currently proposes
as cheap safety readouts - including the first-token logit-gap margin and a black-box refusal
rate - are pushed from the honest-panel unsafe region past the safe threshold by rung F0 or F1,
on at least four of six honest checkpoints, while the discrimination ground truth moves by less
than a pre-registered equivalence margin. The cost of doing so is filed in seconds, FLOPs and
labelled examples; F0 should be zero on the last two.

H2, the inversion. Spearman correlation between honest-panel predictive accuracy and forgery
cost across the battery is negative with a confidence interval excluding zero, resampling over
checkpoints and clustered by lineage, with both aggregation units reported. A correlation that
is merely non-positive is reported as a weaker version of the same claim; a clearly POSITIVE
correlation refutes H2 and is itself worth reporting, because it would mean the field's existing
selection rule is accidentally also selecting for robustness.

H3, the functional-form prediction. The MARGINAL versus CONDITIONAL label, filed before any
forgery was run, predicts measured forgery cost better than chance - a rank-biserial effect with
a confidence interval excluding zero - and in particular no metric labelled MARGINAL survives
past F2. If conditional metrics fall just as cheaply, the mechanism is refuted and the honest
finding is that all cheap safety metrics are forgeable for a few seconds of compute, which is a
strong and directly actionable negative.

H4, the audit and its transfer. (a) The off-manifold residual, fitted on honest checkpoints only,
separates forged from honest checkpoints with held-out-family AUROC at least 0.85 and strictly
above the best single metric's, with a specificity floor set so that no honest checkpoint of an
unseen family is flagged. (b) The payoff: on real un-forged Hugging Face checkpoints the same
residual correlates with the in-distribution to out-of-distribution safety retention ratio at
rho of at least 0.5, and beats the black-box discrimination baseline computed at the same prompt
budget in a paired comparison.

DISCONFIRMED, and each way is reportable. If every metric including the conditional ones falls
at F0, the enterprise of cheap safety metrics for untrusted checkpoints is refuted outright and
the deliverable is the ladder plus that result. If no cheap forgery moves the metrics at all -
that is, the ladder simply fails to fool anything - then cheap safety metrics are already
tamper-resistant and the correct conclusion is that the honest-panel selection rule was fine all
along. If H4(a) succeeds but H4(b) fails, the residual is a tamper detector and not a safety
metric, which must be said plainly: it would mean forged safety and shallow safety are NOT the
same signature, and the unification claim is wrong.

PRE-EMPTIVE CONTROLS, without which none of the above counts. A random-direction null matched for
anisotropy, not an isotropic one. Whole-family holdout for every threshold and every fitted
manifold. A metric is never evaluated on a checkpoint used to choose its layer or its threshold.
The abliterated arm is a known-unsafe anchor, not a label to be predicted, and any metric that
separates it trivially because abliteration is a rank-1 edit is flagged as reading the edit
rather than the risk. Every claim of the form "beats the black-box baseline" is a paired test at
matched prompt budget, because an advantage that disappears once the baseline is given the same
number of prompts is not an advantage.
EOF
wc -c success.txt
```

### [103] TOOL CALL — Bash · 2026-09-20 20:20:54 UTC

```
Draft technical terms:
cat > terms.txt <<'EOF'
Cheap safety metric===A number estimating how safe a model is, computed from a single checkpoint in seconds to a couple of minutes, using its weights, a few dozen short forward passes, or a handful of generations - with no parent model, no attested base to compare against, and no full benchmark run.
Forgery cost===The cheapest rung of a fixed, cost-ordered ladder of edits to a checkpoint that pushes a given metric into its "safe" region while the model's true safety, measured as discrimination between harmful and benign-but-alarming requests, does not improve. Recorded in three units: wall-clock seconds, training FLOPs, and number of labelled examples needed.
Forgery ladder===Five rungs of increasing cost used as the common yardstick: F0 an edit to files that ship in the repository such as the chat template or generation config, costing zero FLOPs; F1 an inference-time system prompt; F2 a training-free rank-1 injection of a refusal direction into the weights; F3 a rank-4 keyword-triggered refusal adapter trained for about a hundred steps; F4 genuine light safety fine-tuning on real paired data, which does buy some real safety and therefore caps the ladder.
Refusal injection===The exact inverse of abliteration: instead of projecting a refusal direction out of the weights, add it back in. Needs no training data and no parent model, and makes an arbitrary checkpoint refuse.
Marginal statistic===A metric that is a level, a norm, a rate, or an unconditional propensity - a fixed functional of the model evaluated without reference to how it varies across inputs. Any such linear functional of the residual stream can be set to an arbitrary value by a rank-1 additive weight edit, which is why these are predicted to be the cheap-to-forge ones.
Conditional statistic===A metric defined by the relationship between two internal quantities across items within one model - for instance, how much of the variance in the model's refusal drive is explained by its own internal estimate of how harmful the request is. Moving one of these requires the relationship to change, not just a level, which is predicted to require actually installing a discriminator.
Discrimination ground truth===The safety label the metrics are graded against: a single score combining graded compliance with harmful requests and false refusal of benign requests that merely look alarming, constructed so that a model refusing everything scores badly rather than perfectly.
Retention ratio===A checkpoint's out-of-distribution safety divided by its in-distribution safety, measured on the same harmful items presented plainly versus wrapped, paraphrased or translated. The quantity a downloader actually cares about and the one a few-prompt behavioural score cannot estimate without running the out-of-distribution set.
Honest metric manifold===The low-dimensional set that the metric vectors of honestly-trained checkpoints occupy, because all their coordinates are downstream of one training process. Fitted on honest checkpoints only; a forged checkpoint has one coordinate moved and the rest unchanged, so it sits off this set.
Off-manifold residual===The distance of a checkpoint's metric vector from the honest manifold. The proposed audit statistic - a consistency check among readouts rather than the level of any one of them.
Abliteration===A training-free community edit that removes a linear refusal direction from a model's weights, producing an "uncensored" checkpoint. Used here as the known-unsafe anchor at the opposite end of the ladder from refusal injection.
EOF
awk -F'===' '{print NF-1}' terms.txt | sort -u; wc -l terms.txt
```

Looking inside buys fewer prompts, not better answers===At a matched prompt budget, an activation readout is a lower-variance estimator of the SAME safety quantity a black-box score estimates, so internals win only in the few-prompt regime and the advantage disappears as the budget grows. Concretely: with at most eight prompts, the best activation readout predicts the full benchmark discrimination score better than any behavioural estimator built from the same eight prompts, and the two curves cross by roughly sixty-four prompts.===This wins if the real reason internal readouts have failed to beat black-box baselines is that every published comparison gave the baseline an unlimited prompt budget. The world would have to be one where refusal is essentially one-dimensional and behaviourally visible, so no unique construct lives inside the model - but where binary generation outcomes are so noisy that a graded continuous read of the same decision is worth many samples. That makes cheapness, not validity, the thing internals buy, which is a cleaner and more immediately useful answer than the main hypothesis if no metric turns out to be forgery-resistant.
Safety depth predicts what survives distribution shift===The layer depth at which a checkpoint's refusal signal becomes decodable and locks onto its harm representation - readable from one model with no reference - predicts the ratio of its out-of-distribution to in-distribution safety, while every behavioural score predicts only the in-distribution level. A model whose refusal is resolved early, before the request is semantically represented, is running a lookup and loses most of its safety under wrapping, paraphrase or translation; one that resolves late is running a decision and retains it.===This wins if the forgery framing turns out to be unnecessary because REAL checkpoints already span the whole shallow-to-deep range, so the interesting variance exists without constructing any fakes. It would beat the main hypothesis in a world where deliberate metric-gaming is rare but accidental shallow alignment is everywhere, which is plausible given how many Hub checkpoints come from light fine-tunes and merges. It is also the cleaner claim if the off-manifold residual turns out to detect edits without tracking any real safety property.
The model-level score is the wrong target===No cheap metric can beat the architecture-family label at ranking whole models, because published work already shows within-family safety variance is near zero while families differ sharply. The defensible and more useful claim is item-level: from a single forward pass on a given prompt, an internal readout predicts whether THIS checkpoint will comply with THAT harmful request, at an accuracy no model-level score can reach, and the per-model average of those item predictions recovers the benchmark score as a by-product.===This wins if the ceiling on model-level prediction is structural rather than a failure of the readouts, which the near-zero within-family variance result directly implies. The world would have to be one where the useful safety signal is genuinely conditional on the input, so aggregating it away is what destroyed it. It would beat the main hypothesis by relocating the whole enterprise rather than adding an axis to it, and it disagrees sharply: under this alternate, the main hypothesis's honest metric manifold is mostly a manifold of family identity.
The activation metamodel is reading lineage, not safety===A small regressor trained on pooled hidden states predicts benchmark safety scores better than any hand-written formula, and the reason is that it recovers architecture and lineage identity, not safety. Ablation predicts the advantage collapses to nothing once whole families are held out, while a lineage-identity probe on the same features stays near perfect.===This wins as the right answer if the strongest rival to every formula is a learned metamodel and nobody has isolated what it reads. It beats the main hypothesis in a world where the metamodel genuinely does generalise to unseen families, which would mean a safety signal exists in activations that no hand-designed statistic has captured - and the main hypothesis's whole framing, built on interpretable named metrics, would be looking in the wrong place.
EOF
