  0.5*subspace-overlap; HRCI_full adds a layer-proximity term (0.4/0.4/0.2). **Its own conclusion is
  adverse and should be ADOPTED, not argued with**: SFT is a negative control whose coupling barely
  moves while ASR stays high, so "low coupling is not itself a safety score"; coupling is "a
  descriptive diagnostic rather than a standalone safety predictor". Mistral-7B trajectories only.
  => **coupling-as-accuracy is CLOSED; coupling-as-forgery-resistant-functional-form is open.**

  fragility score, 21 models, predicts refusal survival after FUTURE fine-tuning), and **2607.12792
  "Silent Alarm" J-space protocol**. All three are LEVELS at fixed coordinates => H3 predicts their
  forgery cost in advance, and **none has ever been run against an edited checkpoint.**
- **GAP CONFIRMED EMPTY by a dedicated search:** no cheap weights/activations metric anywhere is
  validated against a model-level ground truth that PENALISES A BLANKET REFUSER. Silent Alarm
  reports over-refusal only as a side column and states a hard-refusing Gemma-9B scores only
  "marginally lower" on its internal metric. IRT work (2608.05086 / 2606.20626) is the one place
  the two sides are combined — by sign-flipping OR-Bench-Hard — but it is fully behavioural.
- **No paper turns harm/refusal coupling into a PER-CHECKPOINT number** (2507.11878, 2607.00572
  HARC, 2609.14759, 2603.05773 all item-level or training interventions).
- **"refusal injection" / "inverse abliteration" has NO paper** — only community wikis.

Qwen/Qwen3-4B-SafeRL, mlabonne/Qwen3-4B-abliterated, huihui-ai/Huihui-Qwen3-4B-abliterated-v2,

**google/gemma-2-2b-it still gated=manual**. Datasets 200: walledai/XSTest, bench-llm/or-bench,
Uppaal/OpenSafeIntent, walledai/AdvBench.

black-box at matched prompt budget); (2) safety DEPTH — layer where refusal decodes vs layer where
content decodes — predicts retention, no forgeries at all; (3) model-level target is wrong, go
ITEM-level; (4) activation metamodel beats formulas but is reading LINEAGE (ablation: collapses
under held-out-family while a lineage probe stays perfect).

### [304] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-20 20:28:02 UTC

```
The hypothesis is written and validated against the schema at `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`.

**What I proposed: "Cheap safety scores are easy to fake."** Seven earlier runs of this same prompt all shipped a new *readout* and all lost to the same baselines — a plain black-box refusal rate and even the model card beat every internal metric at predicting benchmark safety. Rather than propose an eighth readout, this changes the axis a metric is judged on. A cheap metric for a stranger's checkpoint should be ranked by what it costs to **forge**, and that ranking is roughly the reverse of the accuracy ranking.

**The mechanism is algebraic, in three steps that match three edit types.**

- A constant bias added at the coordinate a metric reads sets any *level* to any value, and the identical offset cancels exactly from any statistic computed *across items*.
- A rank-1 edit along one direction can move an across-item statistic, but only by rescaling a signal the model already carries, so it can amplify or erase a harm-conditioned response and cannot create one.
- That cap is loose on plain harmful prompts, because harm is nearly linearly separable in almost every pretrained model. So the rank-1 forgery buys real but *shallow* safety, which dies under wrapping or translation. Measuring the same statistic on perturbed items closes that route and leaves only real training.

Forgery cost and safety depth turn out to be the same axis, which makes the payoff claim a consequence rather than a hope: the check that catches a deliberate fake should also grade how much of a genuine model's safety survives distribution shift.

**Verification I did myself.** All cited arXiv identifiers were checked against their live abstract pages, and one title was corrected. The whole Qwen3-4B panel plus six honest lineages and four prompt datasets were confirmed ungated. A dedicated prior-art probe established that two works usually listed as cheap safety metrics are not reference-free at all, that the three that are all read levels at fixed coordinates, that none has ever been run against an edited checkpoint, and that no cheap weights-or-activations metric anywhere is validated against a target a blanket refuser loses on. The nearest neighbour on gaming an internal readout attacks a per-input monitor at inference, not a model-level certificate.

Four alternates are carried: internals buy sample efficiency rather than validity; refusal depth predicts shift retention with no forgeries at all; the model-level target is wrong and the work belongs at item level; and the activation metamodel wins by reading lineage.

Two of the three prior-art probes I launched had not returned when I finished; the third delivered the landscape map above, and I ran roughly fifteen searches and six full-text fetches directly.
```
